ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e7763	Next speaker: Mrs Dr. Karl. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e7784	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! The present legislative amendment strengthens the Court of Auditors' responsibility by extending its control competence on the one hand. This is, of course, to be welcomed. On the other hand, however, it must not be overlooked that this does not solve all the problems. The “real mastery” is an undetermined concept of law, whose interpretation is left to the Court of Auditors and the Constitutional Court. Of course, the question arises here when to assume actual mastery or, vice versa, when such a one is to be denied. So far, a government has been adopted by the public authorities and thus an audit of the Court of Auditors only after a 50 % participation by the public authorities or in one of these equal powers. Now the word “real” is added to the mastery – a small word with great effect, especially with a large scope for interpretation. According to the report of the Constitutional Committee, there should be effective mastery if, due to the financial, legal and factual circumstances, it is clear that the federal government alone or together with other legal entities subject to the control of the Court of Auditors dominates the undertaking. The phrase ‘financial, legal and factual circumstances’ refers to a taxative, i.e. a final listing of these three criteria. Other than the legislative text, according to which effective control is expressed by financial or other economic or organisational measures. On the other hand, what these financial, economic and organisational measures have to look like in concrete terms is not explained in detail and therefore requires an in-depth examination in each specific case. In my opinion, one thing is clear: A review of those companies in which the State holds shares but does not have a factual participation shall not take place. Similarly, no detailed publication of business interna or strategic and business decisions taken in the company may be subject to a public audit by the Court of Auditors. Such disclosure would lead to competitive disadvantages and, of course, to a risk to the economic success of companies. Mr Klubobmann Cap has already mentioned the importance of legal certainty and investment security. This is also clearly and clearly reflected in the report of the Constitutional Committee. This report mentions the need for an understanding of ‘real mastery’, of legal certainty for investors, and of avoiding doubts. This important premise must be taken into account by the Court of Auditors and the Constitutional Court if it is to be judged whether there is actual control within the meaning of the law. It will, of course, also be the Court of Auditors to apply these new provisions in a reasonable and careful manner and not to request an examination on call. We shall transfer to the Court of Auditors with our present a decision has a significant degree of responsibility, namely a responsibility to deal with the new provision carefully. My previous speakers have already expressed several words of respect and recognition to the Court of Auditors. I also trust in the work of the Court of Auditors and its competence. He has already proved on many occasions that he is a central institution of Parliament and is careful to exercise his competences. I also place this confidence in the Constitutional Court, which will undoubtedly succeed in developing a strengthened Judicatur in its interpretation of “real rule” and thus creating legal certainty. Thank you.

Of these, approximately €85 billion were earmarked for liabilities and €15 billion for participation capital. In participation capital, almost 11 billion have already been allocated. I believe that this measure was really important. It will now be examined whether the strict requirements of this bank package, these contracts concluded with the individual banks, are respected, including with regard to lending to the Austrian economy. In addition to the other control bodies such as the financial market supervision or the Ministry of Finance or the FIMBAG, the Court of Auditors may, today's application, examine the contracts concluded under this framework. I think we have the biggest problems to face a financial crisis of this magnitude, which we have experienced in the last twelve, thirteen, fourteen months. Let us make it clear that the rest of the world is acting the way Austria has done. With control and transparency, participants in the financial market will be able to do everything better and easier. Thank you.

Let's remember: This bank package consists of several articles. Among other things, it is about taking over liability, in the second part it is about strengthening equity, which was precisely the dispute between Mr Zanger and Mr Bartenstein. By the way, it was also a matter of security of deposit. Everything in one package. First of all, I would like to limit myself to the two main points of liability and equity. Liabilities: To do this, you have to say that the money – and only then – will be staggering if the liability becomes due until there is a liability fee. So far, so good, the basic construction is certainly not wrong. We have also seen that this is not at all needed to the intended extent, it was a shield. That is why, I believe, it was reasonable, precisely from this point of view, to “book away ” the €10 billion for industrial liability. The fact that too little has been done there has led to individual Members and political groups not agreeing to it again, but it was reasonable to take it away because we could already see that the shield in this size was not needed at least for the time being. On the role of the Court of Auditors: What's there to check? – It will be possible to examine whether and to what extent the fees of these liability fees have corresponded exactly to the situation and what the law actually provides for them. The law itself will only be able to question the Court of Auditors insofar as it recommends that we should change something there and there, as there would have been major problems in implementing it. Otherwise, it will be a matter of checking whether the executive is speaking: essentially the Ministry of Finance – by the way, in union with the Federal Chancellery – which has implemented this way. But I do not see in this part of the liability, as has been said, the major problem at all; but this is, in any case, just a presumption; it should all be examined, that is, the intention of the request. Second point: Capitalisation. - Yeah, that's it. Article 2 deals essentially with this, but also with other things. There, of course, it is already more interesting when it comes to certain banks receiving this capital increase, but it is precisely and precisely as participation capital. What does that mean? – No corresponding right of participation. And then, when zero reports or losses are in the balance sheet, there are no repayments to the state either. That is why the term “interests” is wrong. The liberals have now constantly mounted and asked why there are no interest rates. – That's why it was done exactly that way. That's what I think I'm doing. One cannot demand from someone who is supposed to be supported, such as the banks, that in the event of loss he pay something else. So we can just throw away the entire bank package; I'm not. The accident is first of all the capital of participation. Secondly, however, there is a very different problem: Why has and will become in such a case – and that is still Worse – without a right of change? In any event, we do not know from media reports in the case of Raiffeisen and the First Bank that this right of change has been waived. You can even make the balance sheets every year in such a way that they have zero profits, then again without automatic change taking place in this case – the banks fear like the devil the water of consecration! It's all the better if we had state capital there. Thus, because they fear it, they would have an incentive to balance and behave accordingly. I think that's a fundamental mistake. ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e10943	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! In this proposal we deal with the reorganisation and efficiency of the examination of the conduct of the communities. This is in contrast to the way in which some local government representatives have formulated, not an attack on local self-government, but we set ourselves here in this House the goal of developing a test concept within six months, which includes how communities can be checked at different levels. There are many levels of testing, and we believe that a well-coordinated mix can be an efficient instrument that could also give the communities the possibility of consulting. There are already several audit bodies in the municipalities: the audit committees, the control office, the internal revision, the municipal departments of the countries, the national accounting offices, the financial office, the social security and then also the district headquarters. The mayors here in the High House are always very striking that there cannot be an additional level of examination, because there are already a lot of levels, but that an efficient design of these exam possibilities is important. There are examples of communities where the financial performance is so overwhelming that they can no longer carry out the tasks for citizens. These communities are known – an illustrated one in our country has published a list. It is therefore not a matter of examining just the purpose of self-esteem, but it is important to find an instrument to be able to produce an example so that a community can learn from the other community how the services for citizens can be organised as well as possible. In our opinion, no limit should be fixed on the population and population numbers or on the budget figures. For us, it is important that all communities can be examined once or at all. Since I hear the cry coming: Then the Court of Auditors or any other body is in every municipality in Austria every year!, I would like to say: My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, you don't need to have this fear, because it's like it's about punctual exams. There is also the proposal to carry out cross-sectional checks on various topics, namely the introduction of a graded possibility of examinations. In the political construction of our state, it must also be seen as a support for the communities, for it is so – and always is formulated in this House – that local communities offer many services and very many important infrastructure measures to citizens on the ground. This strength and strength of the Austrian State as a whole must be preserved. Ladies and gentlemen! Today, we have also been dealing with the Upper Austrian election campaign several times, and I would like to introduce an initiative by the SPÖ Women Upper Austria, which has set itself the goal of bringing 1 200 women into Upper Austria's municipal policy. [[The speaker keeps a folder up to date.]] [[Abg. Scheibner: But this is not necessary!]] I also see this as an important approach. For example, since 1945 the ÖVP in Upper Austria has not even had a woman in the state government, and the other parties look similar. On the question 9: This question is misleading. It is a fundamental consensus of this Federal Government that there will be no sustainable tax or contribution increases. There is therefore no proposal from the Ministry of Health for Increases in Contributions or an additional burden on the insured persons in social health insurance. Question 10: This question 10 is based on an incorrect level of information. The basis for the integrated stationary and outpatient planning of health care is the Austrian Health Structure Plan, adopted by the Federal Health Commission in 2005 and 2006. This ÖSG was subject to a first revision in 2009. The focus is currently on the planning of the outpatient area. In almost all regional health platforms, the regional structural plans have been coordinated health. The parts of the RSGs relevant to hospitals have been largely adopted in the form of regulations. On the question 11: What's this about? – I firmly support the commitment of the Federal Government to secure the solidarity of Austrian healthcare. The main association must work and implement the cost-reduction and mandatory cost-reduction path now presented. We made this very clear in Salzburg. The aim is to achieve the decommissioning of the local health telephone and a balanced delivery of the local health telephone until the end of the legislative period. The Federal Government is in the best way to achieve this through the measures that have been taken so far. It is my job, in annual negotiations with the Federal Minister for Finance, to talk about the donation of the Cash Structure Fund in an adequate amount. I've already done this for 2010. For 2011 and the following years, the subject will be in the budget negotiations. We have introduced e-medication so far that there is a unanimous decision by all those involved in the Federal Health Commission. The Medical Chamber, the Pharmacovigilance Chamber, the hospitals and also the social insurance institutions have jointly agreed to introduce the e-medication as well. With regard to hospital financing, it is about analysing the potential for efficiency and developing appropriate measures, for example through cross-sector funding models that we have already begun. And, of course, financial compensatory negotiations regarding hospital financing do not fail. Questions 12 and 13: The forecast for financing the health system for the years 2013 to 2025, if you have a hint of solidarity, will be based only on the continuation of the development of recent years. Over the last ten years, the share of expenditure on the health system, as measured by gross domestic product, has remained stable and this is the only relevant target. Question 14: I should like to point out once again the measures taken by the Federal Government. Firstly, in order to reorganise the liquidity of the individual local health telephone one-time amount of € 45 million for the regional health insurance fund in 2009, disbanding the tied reserve in the compensation fund, which was the disaster fund of the compensation fund, around € 42 million, allocation to the local health insurance fund. The amount of GSBG funds has been maintained. Secondly, medium-term and long-term rehabilitation or safeguarding of balanced conduct, setting up of the cash fund with the financial year 2010 and the restructuring package of the main association. The third point is the decommissioning of the regional health telephone, which will take place in the years 2010, 2011 and 2012 at the Federal Finance Agency in the amount of €150 million respectively. Question 15: The BZÖ is expected to escape the fact that there was a joint government decision at the Salzburg Declaration, presented by Federal Chancellor Werner Faymann and Vice Chancellor Josef Pröll. It is clear that the payment of € 100 million of the Cash Structure Fund for 2010 is linked to the achievement of the specifically prescribed savings targets. The share debt of € 3 times € 150 million is released in connection and on the basis of the entire restructuring concept of the main association. On the question 16: The so-called disaster fund – in fact, it is a binding reserve within the framework of the regional health telephone compensation fund – was not fed by means of means of taxes, but was fed by contributions from insured persons. Strengthening and improving the leadership or quality of management staff were not the objective of the distribution scheme. A solidaric attitude – and this is the point of a compensation fund when one is in payment difficulties and this cash in payment difficulties is helped – has the potential to strengthen an institution's will for performance and management. On the question 17: The main association of the Austrian social security funds reflects the structure of self-management borne by the social partners as the umbrella organisation of all statutory social security funds. It is therefore the appropriate and responsible organisations to set the course for efficient and effective structures in the established area. This can only be achieved if the individual insurance companies act in common responsibility by defining objectives, are willing to undergo benchmarking, and are also responsible for the outcome of the respective insurance company. The main association has no responsibility for the stationary sector, it lies with the countries, and therefore no mandate has been given to the main association. On the question 18: I have always said that I am at the Rechnungshof, on his duties, as provided for in Article 121 B-VG. In this context, there have been very, very constructive discussions and exchanges of views with my department and the Court of Auditors in the past too, and we have come up with consistent solutions. For example, I should like to remind you of the ideas of the former President of the Court of Auditors, who, in the context of the work in the Constitutional Convention, proposed changes in health skills, which, however, was not used in 2003, when the Convention was set up, and in 2007, when my party took over government responsibility, it was implemented. On question 19: Health policy, especially for patients, includes not only opinions but results. In this context, I should like to refer to the comments made at the beginning of my reply to the question and to what the government is doing. It is also about ensuring health care in the future. I have already pointed out that 80 percent of the population say we have received a good paper. Question 20: It's about the new flu. Due to international findings, we have defined target groups for vaccination against the influenza A virus H1N1. It is first the health care staff to protect already sick and therefore endangered patients from infections. Then people should go to 49. years of life are vaccinated with certain chronic basic diseases belonging to a risk group. Sufficient vaccine is available. Once this vaccine is approved in Austria, we can also recommend vaccination to these target groups. The immediate consultation must be carried out by the family doctor or by the vaccine doctor specifically for the person concerned. On question 21: In Austria, only a vaccine will be used that does not contain adjuvants, i.e. enhancers, preservatives or nanoparticles. That was also the basis for my decision on this vaccine. On the question 22: The question shows that the applicant does not know the cost development in the pharmaceutical sector for 2009. In this year's social security sector, we are already looking at the VAT effect adjusted from a cost increase of just under 1.8 percent. That's the lowest value for many years. The debate on the cash register has already borne its fruit. The AGES shall review samples drawn by the food inspectors of the countries. If the AGES experts object to samples, the countries will again be on the train. It is not one of the real tasks of the AGES to look for shy ham or analogue cheese or to keep it away from the market. The AGES is a service provider and provider of analysis and assessment orders from the Ministry or the countries. I have commissioned the AGES with a special evaluation regarding artificial cheese. The result: For 60 samples of cheese or cheese foods, there was only a falsification or an error in four cases. Therefore, the exposure rate is lower than the average exposure rate for all foods. [[Congratulations on MEPs from ÖVP and SPÖ.]] One thing has to be said, ladies and gentlemen, and I am saying this because we here in this House will often discuss health policy: I would like to point out at five points the enormous challenges facing a health minister – a health minister who has few competences, but who is responsible for everything. First point. There are few as sensitive areas as health. In the needs scale, probably from all of you, health comes up quite, very high. That is to say, one has to deal with this subject very carefully, because one cannot make political rapid shots, because one cannot proceed with the bridge rod. There is a need to make careful, continuous and reliable policy. Second point: Things are changing constantly in health care – the Minister pointed out. Everything is constantly on the move, on the supply side and on the demand page. On the offer page: More and more, better and more expensive medical services are being offered. On the demand side is the age structure of the population, every ten years life expectancy increases by two years. This is a huge challenge from the demand side. There are increasing traffic and sports accidents, many other things becoming demand-effective, i.e. disproportionate cost increases. Third point: The challenge is a double one, ladies and gentlemen. The challenge is: Quality assurance while at the same time reducing costs, which is almost the square of the circle. One could make quality assurance very easy if one did not have to take into account the costs, one could very easily reduce costs if one did not have quality assurance. But the combination of both objectives is the square of the circle. Many previous speakers have already said that we have a health system in Austria, which is always among the top 3 worldwide in every international comparison. As far as patient satisfaction is concerned, too, we have a high level of patient satisfaction with our health system. Ladies and gentlemen! This is a value in itself, some previous speakers have already pointed out, including Erwin Rasinger. I would not like to get sick in any other country in the world and be left there to the health system! If I was abroad and would be ill, I would fly back to Austria immediately because I have the best medical care here. Fourth point: Especially in the health field, the purely isolated view of experts and the political view often disperse diametrically. I cite a concrete example from my Waldviertel constituency. For years, the Hospitals Allensteig and Eggenburg had the footnote “for closure. ” I couldn't blame any purely health expert to say: What do we need these two hospitals for? However, politics had to say – and there were all political groups in the forest district – in addition to health policy, there is a regional policy, a workplace-based, a value-added aspect and there are future trends needed. What did we do? – The sites were preserved, but the one as a clinic for psychosomatics and the other as a neurorehabilitation. And today both clinics have waiting lists, so attractive. Of course, if we could have reduced costs, we would have closed both locations, but that would have been at the expense of quality medical care. Fifth and final point, Minister: I have just mentioned this: I don't envy a health minister. Little competence, the countries have the say at the hospitals, but they are made responsible for everything. Not a fun job! Mr Stöger, of course, You're judged by the performance – of us as your coalition partner, of your own party, of the population, but I think it's absurd to say after ten months of a current account, given the things I've mentioned: And now the Minister is being exchanged. This is the wrong way, ladies and gentlemen. I say again: We need continuity, reliability and sustainability. That is why we are for Minister Stöger.

ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e17645	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Last Friday we discussed very intensively and quite controversially on the subject of eco electricity law. I would like to take the opportunity to address some of the very important aspects that have certainly been the subject of Friday. The Eco electricity Act has not yet entered into force, not because the Federal Government would have been poor or because anyone else would have pursued a blockade policy, but because we had to do it with a very lengthy examination procedure of the European Commission. It has already been noted before the summer that information about what happens to our eco electricity law may be relevant. Finally, the Commission's opinion came only during the summer and the repair of the criticised parts was started. This opinion is different from what was originally adopted. Nevertheless, there have been points to look at and agree on new solutions. I would like to make it clear once again that from the very first moment all the parties involved have started to hold the talks and also to ensure that it is coming quickly to this novel, to this change in the eco electricity law. On the one hand, it is about confirming the law that has already been adopted, and on the other hand, it is about repairing the points that have been criticized accordingly. I am also very pleased that, in the course of these negotiations and discussions, we have succeeded in ensuring that we can decide on this eco-strom law in the House today with a relatively broad decision-making process. Initial intention in connection with the so-called “Industrial Cover” was to relieve energy-intensive plants financially. That is exactly the point that has been criticized by the Commission and is now subject to a main audit procedure. We cannot say today whether and when the European Union will give it its way to this regulation, therefore a European-law-based replacement has to be drawn up. This replacement is here now. It is a transitional solution, and industry will not get as much as it would have been planned in the industrial cover. It is therefore also important that we monitor this main audit procedure closely and also Look at how things develop and then quickly reach a final solution – after this procedure has been completed. I also regard the discharge of industry, which we decide today, as a contribution to the security of locations and jobs, because we are still concerned with high-quality and secure jobs. These high-quality and high-quality jobs are achieved, for example, by increasing funds for district heating and cooling. These additional funds, which will be made available on the initiative of social democracy, are first of all an important signal to strengthen the economy and second, will allow savings in CO2 emissions. Eco electricity law and management are important elements of a sustainable energy policy. I am very pleased that we are able to take these decisions accordingly today. - Thank you.

This is in about the same way as it was implemented in Germany. Since we're in close interdependence, it's very important. As the main audit procedure has been initiated by the EU, the consequence is that, if it is still in the test, we cannot put the other part into effect. On the one hand, I am very grateful to the industry for the gnawing – I must say, really gnawing – that it has understood that we are starting with this part of the implementation now and on the other hand, with the motion for a resolution, we also have a possibility to establish an EU-compliant state aid situation with regard to this point. On the other hand, I am also very grateful to those who are responsible for the decision-making today. That is why my respect for the FPÖ applies. My thanks to our clubman Karlheinz Kopf, but also to our coalition partner, because it is very important that we start implementation. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP]] After all, what is the result? – If we did not, we would have a major audit procedure, which does not bring about anything for anyone, and so we will start investing in the 3 billion area as a whole, as far as the year 2015 is concerned. We're doing something for the small hydropower. We're doing something for the photovoltaics. As far as the motion for a resolution is concerned, I believe that it is a right variant to increase funding over the KLI.EN. It is also possible to start with the level of funding, to achieve better coordination with the national organisations, and thus possibly to allow more promoters to move, because I think the way through project promotion is the right one from the point of view of implementation. The stronger we go into the collective sector, the less pressure there is towards technology and the less will move on the market. That is why this path, which is now under consideration, is exactly the right one to bring us there. That's the one point. The second point: I was at the BZÖ Club yesterday, and there was a very subject-oriented discussion. I regret that there is no agreement, because on the other hand, this has been linked to the problem, which is the recalculation of costs by the individual producers. – I have assured – that is not my job, but that of the competition authority – that this will be taken into account. The point is – that's why it's so hard to understand – that the question arises: What is the cheapest offer and what is the settlement price? – You have to look at that in practice. It will be duly detectable, and then there will be the corresponding consequences. Mr Scheibner, I would like to have the reservation of innocence before that, if someone says: That is not so!, also has the opportunity to represent the opposite. That is also the case in this case. The next point – which the first speaker raised shortly before the urgent question – concerns the question or the claim that we are now granting a huge chance to implement climate change targets with the unfulfilled eco-strom law. I would, without devaluing the ecostrom law now, relativize a little. Ladies and gentlemen, are you aware that we have around 20 percent of total energy consumption? – 20 percent of total energy consumption the share of electricity, and of this share of electricity is currently about 8 per cent of the aided share, if you take away the hydropower, small power plants. That's relatively little. We have also tried to calculate it differently. On the 34 percent target of renewable energy, the eco electricity sector, the funded electricity sector, has about 6 percent. That is why it is important to do something about it. As far as the CO2 targets are concerned, the 3 million tonnes have already been addressed, we can ensure that they are significantly affected. But the crucial point, ladies and gentlemen, is that of efficiency. The crucial point lies in the heat, which we blow everything into the air, and the crucial point is in the area of car transport. There we will be able to achieve the climate change objectives, and there we need to be even more rescheduled. But I would warn against mixing Kyoto with the one goal too much. It is important that we release this objective once. In this context, the question has always been put into space: Why and why don't you just write down the German energy law in the area and then have exactly the same advantages? – Because implementation would take about two years to transfer this system to us. However, the problem in the German field is another one. They have no hydropower, so they count the eco-strom costs directly into the price. It's not particularly noticeable there either. If, on the other hand, we would do so without limit in the price, then we would actually have to drive out the market without the cover of the other energy sources. Then we would have fewer windwheels, less biogas, less in other areas, including hydropower. It is therefore important to ensure balanced conditions in the interests of the consumer. I think we do. Nobody closes to the fact that photovoltaics, solar energy is the energy of the future. But it must be that our possibilities – and also those of the consumer, including those of the other alternative energy producers – are in line with the promotions. That, I think, has been quite successful with this law. I have nothing against it, which has been tabled as a resolution here, either, that we say: Let's look at the system! Let's rework it! We can also take with us the aid problems of large firms. We are now helping them with the de minimis rule. However, there are still 15 holdings left, which are practically disproportionately burdened by costs. Ladies and gentlemen, it would be bad if it were a law, in which everyone would just say that's the optimal, because someone might be disadvantaged. It is a compromise, as many other novels do. I would like to thank all those involved who are now helping to implement it, and would like to join in exactly what you have said, Mr Hofer: In the final effect, it is much better that we now put the one part into practice and thus help those who need it than when we discuss forever, decide nothing and thus have nothing. So something's better than nothing. - Thank you.

The most important point is the question of reimbursement, because it actually regulates the needs previously regulated by the industrial cover. ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e23188	We now come to points 7 and 8 of the agenda on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mrs Schönpass. Fixed speaking time: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e23215	Mr President-in-Office of the Council. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) I refer to the Court of Auditors' partial report on whether the commitments under the Kyoto Protocol can be met. The period under review covered the years 2002 to 2007. The report and the discussion in the Committee of the Court of Auditors made it clear that we are far from achieving the Kyoto objective of reducing greenhouse emissions by 13 percent in 1990. This can and will lead to very far-reaching problems. On the one hand, it is all about the habitat, health and quality of life of the Austrians, and on the other hand, the failure to comply with the Kyoto objective could lead to an EU infringement procedure. And the consequences of this could be that the difference and a third of more greenhouse gases would have to be reduced during the following period, or Austria could be obliged to make payments of €100 per tonne missing. This would then mean a payment of about € 1 billion. Ladies and gentlemen! In order to successfully implement the climate strategy in Austria, numerous measures are necessary. Among other things, we need the involvement and participation of the countries and communities. In the area of transport there is some work ahead of us. However, we also need to talk, for example, about biomass combustion plants. Although these achieve CO2 reduction, they also produce higher fine dust exposure. The thermal renovation of older houses is rather tough, although a massive energy saving could be achieved in this area. However, there are also real demonstration projects. 25 percent of the Federal Rehabilitation Funds go to Upper Austria, and this has a reason: The social democratic housing councillor Hermann Kepplinger launched a rehabilitation offensive in March of this year. Key items are the creation of cash promotion and the elimination of the income limit for thermal rehabilitation. In mid-April, the federal funding system started in parallel, which was also applied massively. And this has led to the fact that the Upper Austrian Energy Saving Association can hardly save itself from consultation and that 25 percent of the federal funding goes to Upper Austria. ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e23276	Mr President! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! The Court of Auditors reviewed the concrete implementation of the Austrian climate strategy at federal level. The aim of the review was to assess whether the commitments under the Kyoto Protocol can be met. In order to prevent climate change, 184 countries worldwide have committed themselves to significantly reducing the six environmentally relevant pollutants. The European Union has set a reduction target of 8 per cent across Europe. Austria has committed itself to a minimum of 13 percent of greenhouse gas emissions in the course of a contract under international law. It is important to note that the Kyoto Protocol provides that sanctions are to be expected if the reduction obligation is not complied with. As a result of the development of emissions, particularly in the space heating, small consumption and transport sectors, the Court of Auditors had doubts about the achievement of the objective. [[Abg. Dr. Moser: Not just doubt! It's inside that they won't be reached!]] Considering the various sectors critically, it must be noted that the highest emissions increase through the transport sector. Positive in terms of achieving the emission targets are the areas of agriculture and waste management. Ladies and gentlemen! The reduction of energy consumption and the increased use of renewable energy sources are key challenges. The most environmentally friendly energy is the one that is saved. Therefore, the focus must be on thermal renovation and energy saving. In the second economic package, €100 million was agreed for the thermal renovation of households and commercial buildings: €50 million for private thermal renovation and €50 million of incentives for projects for thermal rehabilitation in the business sector. In the private sector alone, €400 million of additional investment is being initiated, mainly for the benefit of the regional economy. A total of 5.3 million tonnes of CO2 can be expected to be saved together with operational thermal rehabilitation. Ladies and gentlemen, the majority of the funding in the field of environmental protection in the country, called UFI, was awarded in 2008 for climate protection projects, which means a reduction of 11.7 million tonnes of CO2 in harmful gases. This is expected to be the period of use of the plants. [[President Mag. Prammer is taking over the presidency again.]] As a Waldviertler Member of Parliament and Bauer, I am particularly pleased that the use of €11 million has initiated approximately 16 000 pellet heating – an excellent form of energetic supply of objects. ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e24069	Mr Singer is now speaking. I wish you every time: 3 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2009-09-23-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00037_d8e24090	Madam President. Mr President of the Court of Auditors. (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) In my speech, I am dealing with the Court of Auditors' report on the emissions trading system for greenhouse gases in Austria. The aim was to review the effectiveness of the system and the benefits for the achievement of the Austrian climate target from its implementation. The certification trade was a pilot phase between 2005 and 2007. In Austria 197 installations were included. 33 million certificates were issued annually. The Court praised the transparent and objective allocation. In the industry sector, however, there was an oversupply of certificates across Europe, resulting in an EU-wide price drop of €20 to €7 cents per piece. This resulted in small additional costs for the companies that had to buy allowances and lacked incentives to reduce emissions. Austria is not in debt, however, because as one of only four EU countries, together with Ireland, Spain and the UK, our country has issued fewer certificates than it needs. That is to say, the allocation plan has been drawn up exactly. This agreement was also recognised by the Court of Auditors. However, 5 percent more allowances have been issued across the EU than have actually been issued. This situation led to the price drop already mentioned. For the second period 2008 to 2012, 30.7 million allowances are issued annually. That's by 8 percent less than in the previous period. This reduction in the number of allowances has proved successful throughout Europe. The price is currently around 15 percent, i.e. a positive trend. It is also gratifying that the sample-type review of the emissions trading register by the Federal Environment Agency has resulted in a broad consensus with the actual emissions, which confirms the confidence in our businesses. Ladies and gentlemen, the Court of Auditors has made a number of proposals for improvement, such as the inclusion of other major issuers, such as air transport or the exemption of small plants; these are installations with less than 10 000 tonnes of CO2 emissions per year. It is particularly welcome that the implementation of key recommendations from the Court of Auditors is already planned for the next period after 2012. In summary, however, it is important to make stronger efforts worldwide and thus also in Austria in order to achieve the Kyoto objectives. The instrument of emissions trading is an aspect of this. – Thank you very much.

The European Natura 2000 project is a very important step in European environmental policy, which has already been implemented by my Tyrolean colleague, because, on the one hand, it is intended to prevent the deterioration of the species of flora and fauna and, on the other, to ensure an improvement in habitats. This means that the EU Natura 2000 project helps to further enhance our quality of life in Austria and to protect our flora and fauna sustainablely. A central point of this EU project is the ban on deterioration, i.e. prophylacticly everything must be done to ensure that in the relevant area no deterioration of habitats could occur. Now, for the implementation of this Directive, not only is a territorial authority responsible or empowered to decide, but nine different. Therefore, there has also been a very long or incomplete implementation. Nine different bodies have just needed longer, and the European Commission has initiated infringement proceedings because of insufficient legal implementation. In addition, it is also true that different shares have been nominated in the six federal states audited by the Court of Auditors. For example, the Federal Land of Burgenland has nominated 27 percent of its area, Lower Austria 23 and Carinthia and Upper Austria only 6 percent. In other words, the division of competence in implementation has led to time-consuming, but above all, to unwarranted decisions. These decision-making structures – I believe – must in any case be critically questioned and discussed within the framework of the forthcoming administrative and constitutional reform. Finally, I would like to point out, as Vienna's Member of the Danube City, that the popular area of nature conservation or local recreation is Lobau, which is a protected area of Europe and is also subject to this Natura 2000 regulation, the best policy of the Vienna in the area of nature conservation and environmental policy, since the state of Vienna is not subordinate to the EU's infringement procedure here. Thank you.

I have sometimes seen clear differences in the practical implementation with us. It is not a court hearing, even if the progress of dealing with the issues is quite judicial. We will have to discuss organ disputes. The further development of the Austrian constitutional jurisdiction, clear procedural rules and data protection – has been addressed today – will help us to develop further. It is important, ladies and gentlemen, that we are just trying to carry on with consensus the issue of the development of our rules of the game. Then I'll be we are also making further progress in what is expected of us – objectivity, transparency and speed in dealing with the issues we have to deal with. Thank you very much.

There is also an appropriate working committee under the leadership of the First President with the political groups. The aim is to discuss a common future procedure for handling immunity, including on the basis of experience with the current Committee of Inquiry. I think this procedure is necessary and reasonable.
